Read the 5-word pack with the proven physdump path on a PD4 golden boot (2 s post-release, verified again at 48 s): **CPU 0x10C1B0 (instance+0x20, handler-table pointer) = 0x00118D68 = the image's static value (file 0xCC1B0 contains 0x00118D68 exactly once)**; **the live table at CPU 0x118D68 is ALL ZERO (entries id1/id3/id5 = 0) and stays zero at 48 s** (the boot memset zeroed it and the mailbox registration helper never populated it this session); ROM RAZ window 0x2000 = 0; sanity anchor CPU 0x40000 = 0x00046971 (image loaded correctly). Instance words around it: +0x10 CA 0x10C1A0 = 0x400392D4 (doorbell), +0x14 = 0x40101418, +0x18/+0x1C = 0, +0x20 = 0x00118D68, +0x24 = 0x00360036, +0x28 = 0x001043EA, +0x2C = 0xDEAD4EAD. INTERPRETATION: the PD4 stub's sentinel compare is ALWAYS EQUAL -> it always substitutes the ROM RAZ zero table for the dispatcher call -> **the firmware's mailbox dispatcher handlers are NEVER executed under PD4**; only the ack/clear/doorbell side effects run (and the ROM zeros make the cbz NULL handler checks safe). This REFUTES the earlier assumption (in several lane reports) that PD4 dispatches through the real vendor handler table: the DUPLEX SUCCESS (calibration answers, ctx+0x190 allocation) therefore happens through a NON-MAILBOX path - consistent with the ETE SR engine delivering our H2D frames into device memory and the firmware's pcie_msg_init polled H2D thread processing them (the mailbox ack is bookkeeping). PD4-NOSENT would crash with an empty table; PD4-ALWAYS0 equals the current behavior. This is a citable correction for the RE report (fw-audit H1/H2 answered; re-report2's 6.6 section needs updating). ALSO: bench2 fixed the phase-matrix gate bug (opensource 5d93b4d): the generated gate helpers grepped a hard-coded repo path instead of the passed pattern (template edit artifact) so EVERY attr/output gate failed; now helpers use $p + whitespace normalization, host evaluator _search_pattern_ws, regression tests (selftest 398/398), C2 dry-run PLAN-OK; replay of the failed fire passes load-budget. Root causes found this round: (1) the framework's dw_pcie_setup_rc sets PORT_LOGIC_SPEED_CHANGE after host_init (my earlier clear was overwritten) and runs dw_pcie_dbi_ro_wr_dis() so post-setup DBI writes need 0x8bc-bit0; (2) LTSSM was enabled before the framework's DBI batch (vendor enables it after ALL DBI writes). Both fixed: start_link now does ro_wr_en -> viewports -> clear SPEED_CHANGE -> ro_wr_dis -> LTSSM enable -> steady; post_init retrain is conditional on link_up. Diagnostics: "link training armed DEBUG0=0x003d4900" -> "post-link check DEBUG0=0x001c6200" - LTSSM[5:0] stays 0x00 while DEBUG0 higher fields churn (PHY alive). Live-DTB check: identical (sha 947ec62d; capability=0x03 is a runtime fixup by the vendor driver, not a flash delta). Userspace: gpio_switch empty, hsan scripts write nothing, shuangta branch has no GPIO step. Clock parent verified (pcie0_clk <- apb_clk == LUOFU_SRC_APB). Remaining: PORT_DEBUG0 field decode (ours 0x725000 vs stock-live 0x03f4a611) and the multimeter rail/PERST measurements. | `mem-entries.md:27370` |

| 2026-11-22 |  | 2026-11-22 17:05 - *** BREAKTHROUGH: PCIe LINK TRAINS ON THE 6.18 IMAGE. Root cause was NEVER the PCIe register program - it was BOARD GPIO state. *** Method: built a standalone loadable 6.18 module (luofu_standalone.ko) replicating the vendor hi_pcie.ko sequence with NO DWC framework; it first failed too (link LTSSM 0x00). Then bisected GPIO0 controller state: stock (vendor kernel) drives GPIO0 DDR=0x1330000f / DATA=0x12300004; our image drives DDR=0x0300000f / DATA=0x0300000f. Writing the STOCK values to 0x10106000/0x10106004 and re-running the standalone module: LTSSM=0x11 (L0), DEBUG0=0x03d15111, DEBUG1=0x08000110, and config read cfg[0]=0x000559e7 (the Wi-Fi endpoint answered). Bisect results: DATA bits 0/1/3/24 high are harmless; setting DATA to ours (clearing bits 25/28) DROPS the link; restoring recovers it automatically. DDR: pin21 as output is REQUIRED, pin28 as output is REQUIRED, pin20 output is not. Thus GPIO0 pin28 must be OUTPUT HIGH (PERST deassert / power) and pin21 OUTPUT LOW - the Wi-Fi endpoint's power/reset controls on this board. Our DTS has no node for 20/21/28 (stock kernel configures them). FIX applied in pcie-luofu driver host_init: ioremap 0x10106000, DDR \|= BIT(21)\|BIT(28), DATA = (DATA & ~BIT(21)) \| BIT(28)  -> W25a build. Endpoint config space read live from working link: vendor 0x59e7 device 0x0005, class 0x02800000 (network controller), bars 4/4/4, rev 0xe5. | `mem-entries.md:27398` |
| 2026-11-22 |  | 2026-11-22 17:20 - GPIO root cause pinned down EXACTLY (hardware-verified by runtime bisect on W25a/W25b): the Wi-Fi endpoint (Hi5622/TR5220) needs GPIO0 pins 20, 21 and 28 to be OUTPUTS driven HIGH before PCIe training. Stock (vendor kernel) GPIO0 state: DDR=0x1330000f, DATA=0x12300004 -> output pins 0,1,2,3,20,21,24,25,28 with 20,21,28 all high. Our image (DTS gpio-leds on 0,1,2,3,24,25, no node for 20/21/28) left 20/21/28 as inputs and drove 0,1,3,24 high (LEDs off, active-low). Bisect results: (a) DATA=0x1330000f + DDR=0x1330000f (LEDs-high plus 20/21/28 high) -> LTSSM=0x11 L0, cfg=0x000559e7; (b) DATA=0x12300004 + same DDR -> also trains; (c) any of bits 20/21/28 low (data or direction) -> no link. So fix = ddr \|= BIT(20)\|BIT(21)\|BIT(28) and data \|= BIT(20)\|BIT(21)\|BIT(28) in host_init (plus msleep(1000) settle because our probe is at ~2.5 s while the vendor module runs ~11 s). W25d is the image with this exact fix. | `mem-entries.md:27402` |

| 2026-11-23 |  | 2026-11-23 12:15 - C1 gate-hold test: NEGATIVE, plus a better park-point localisation. Attended session on the recovered box: host warm reboot (B3 rule: no rmmod after release), B3 loaded poll_ms=0, CHIP state before iATU = 0xffff (window not decoding), prepare 6/6 OK, upload 928,920 B, verify diffs=0, P0 arm 7/7 (pre-release DR fill 0x10), release (dmesg CPU release; the release word CA 0x40000108 reads 0x00000000 afterwards = the CPU consumed 0x5a5a). Then sustained gate service (CA 0x4000010C <- 0xcece, ~2,000,000 writes over ~2 min, one per ~100 us) instead of a single write. RESULTS: gate reads 0x0000cece during service (writes land), BUT out[1]=0x00000004 (bit2 only), mask=0x08, g=0, state=1, out[0]/out[2]/out[5]=0 - identical to the single-write park. KEY INSIGHT from the firmware disassembly check: the entry routine sets out[1] = 4 (file 0x86F22-0x86F2C), writes the D2H doorbell (0x86F54-0x86F66), then does out[1] \|= 8 (0x86F68-0x86F70) and ONLY THEN spins on CA 0x4000010C == 0xcece (0x86F74-0x86F7C; literal 0x4000010C at 0x86FA0). We never see bit3 (0x8) in out[1] (it stays 0x4), so the CPU is NOT parked at the gate spin - it parks earlier, most likely inside the calls at 0x86F3E-0x86F50 (bl to 0xfff799c0/0xfff7998e) or the entry-configure loop at 0x86F04-0x86F20. So the C1 hypothesis (sustained 0xcece) is DISPROVEN as the missing action: the spin is never reached. Also confirmed the lane's claim that no vendor host module writes 0xcece (negative control) - the gate value must come from elsewhere/earlier, or the spin is not the blocker at all. NEXT: have boot-kick-re re-analyse where entry 3 actually parks (disassemble 0x86E14-0x86F78 fully, resolve the bl targets, check for hardware waits), and propose the next single-candidate session (likely the L1SS pinmux C2 writes CA 0x40000554/0x400005BC, which our transport never writes). | 2026-11-23 |  | 2026-11-23 19:20 - ROOT-CAUSE HYPOTHESIS FOUND: out[1] bit6 (0x40) = pcie_trigger_ete_sending (firmware asks the host to send ETE/HCC frames; vendor handler pcie_trigger_ete_sending_handle 0x15efc -> pcie_wkup_thread wakes the TX kthread), and out[1] bit2 (0x04) = exception_panic_msg_process (firmware PANIC). Observed timeline on our chip after release: t0.2s out1=0x40 (trigger ETE sending) -> t1.2s out1=0x04 (PANIC) with mask 0x08. Our service loop only DRAINS the bitmap (out[3]=1/out[1]=0/out[4]=1) and never dispatches the per-bit handlers, so the firmware's ETE-send request is ignored, the 1s wait expires and the firmware panics (exception path) => the SVC and the later data abort. The vendor host responds to bit6 by waking its TX thread and sending the ETE frames; its chip never panics (mask=0 golden). CORRECT vendor D2H order (host-replica 4.2): ack *out[3]=1 FIRST, then read bitmap out[1], drain out[1]=0, re-arm out[4]=1, then dispatch bits (id = ctz) with table handlers for ids 1,2,3,4,5,6,7 (1=dev-ready stub, 2=panic, 3=ETE transfer done -> drain DR channels 0x1f, 4/5=PM, 6/7=trigger ETE sending -> wake TX). NEXT: capture the firmware's DR-ring messages (the transport's pciet_d2h_payload copies wire messages into read_msg[core], u16 len at +4, core byte at +9, payload at +0xc) so we can see exactly what the firmware asked for, then implement the bit6 handler = actually send the ETE/HCC frames our transport already has an SR ring for, and re-test whether the firmware avoids the panic (mask stays 0) and the ready exchange (g4 id1/id2) begins. | `mem-entries.md:27550` |

Image header +0x50..0x60 = {0x103140, 0x103114, 0x1030E8, 0x1030BC, 0x103090}; the handler at 0x103140 is a generic entry trampoline that calls blx 0xC2EEC (the event dispatcher). So the ROM's wake/FIQ path enters the image's dispatcher through the header handler. This gives host-only access to the ROM's S0/S1 logic: analyze the 16 KB dump (ARM + Thumb) to find the CPU1-release/S1 condition and any host-triggerable path. | 2026-11-24 |  | 2026-11-24 10:00 - *** ROOT CAUSE FOUND: our 0xCECE gate writes clobber the ROM's 0xCEED boot handshake ***. rom-dump lane (submodule 734696f) fully reversed the 16 KiB mask ROM (2,073 B content 0x0-0x81B: vectors, ARM reset path 0x8C, stack/slot table 0x1E0, Thumb code 0x1FC-0x60B; rest RAZ). S0 = caches/MPU off, per-mode stacks (0x1BF110/0x1BEF00), CP15 c6 MPU-style regions, MPU on, MPIDR split CPU0 primary/CPU1 secondary. Dispatch: CPU0 cold fallback writes 0xBAAD to CA 0x4000010C, analog init, polls [0x40000108]==0x5A5A (host release), writes **0xCEED** to 16-bit CA 0x4000010C, sets page bit8, re-reads [0x40000], blx entry A 0x46970. **CPU1's S1 (mask ROM 0x5FE) writes handshake +8=2 then blx entry B 0xC742C**, after a WFI loop that passes only when [0x1BF00C]==0xABCD AND 16-bit [0x4000010C]==0xCEED. The image never writes 2; entry B is CPU1-dispatched after S1. **EXACT GATE: our transport writes 0xCECE to CA 0x4000010C (BAR0 0x3B810C) as the D2H announce gate and re-writes it continuously, clobbering the ROM's 0xCEED written by the CPU0 fallback at the 0x5A5A release. One 16-bit register carries two mutually exclusive handshakes: ROM boot stage CEED vs image service/exception CECE (image 0xC20F8/0xC6E14 spin for CECE). The vendor never writes CECE and its mask=0, so CEED survives -> golden +8=2.** HOST ACTION (subtractive): release 0x00005A5A -> CA 0x40000108 only; DO NOT write 0xCECE during boot (only after page+8==2 / page+0 bit14 set); optional repair if +8 stays 0: read 16-bit 0x4000010C and if != CEED write 0x0000CEED (CPU1-only consumer). Expected: page 0x86807FEF / 0x7 / 0x2 / 0xABCD; state CA 0x118B60=1; mask 0. Corrections: image header order = {FIQ, data-abort, prefetch-abort, SVC, undefined}; r1 ids undef=1, svc=2, prefetch=3, abort=4 => bench mask 0x0A = undefined+prefetch (NOT svc+data-abort); 0x7386F44F is Thumb code not a magic; 0x1F4/0x1F8 are stack tops; IRQ vector points into image 0x40064. Also explains earlier no-gate-service sessions reaching state=1 (when no CECE clobber). NEXT: GP8 bench - single clean pulse, GP4/GP5 pre-seeding (both custom blocks), upload/verify, release-only with NO 0xCECE writes, read page at 0.2/1/5/20 s + state/mask; repair with CEED if needed. | `mem-entries.md:27650` |

| 2026-11-25 |  | 2026-11-25 21:30 - H1/H2 DECISIVE PACK RESULT: H1 CONFIRMED - PD4's stub is CONSUME-ONLY (the live handler table is EMPTY). Read the 5-word pack with the proven physdump path on a PD4 golden boot (2 s post-release, verified again at 48 s): **CPU 0x10C1B0 (instance+0x20, handler-table pointer) = 0x00118D68 = the image's static value (file 0xCC1B0 contains 0x00118D68 exactly once)**; **the live table at CPU 0x118D68 is ALL ZERO (entries id1/id3/id5 = 0) and stays zero at 48 s** (the boot memset zeroed it and the mailbox registration helper never populated it this session); ROM RAZ window 0x2000 = 0; sanity anchor CPU 0x40000 = 0x00046971 (image loaded correctly). Instance words around it: +0x10 CA 0x10C1A0 = 0x400392D4 (doorbell), +0x14 = 0x40101418, +0x18/+0x1C = 0, +0x20 = 0x00118D68, +0x24 = 0x00360036, +0x28 = 0x001043EA, +0x2C = 0xDEAD4EAD. INTERPRETATION: the PD4 stub's sentinel compare is ALWAYS EQUAL -> it always substitutes the ROM RAZ zero table for the dispatcher call -> **the firmware's mailbox dispatcher handlers are NEVER executed under PD4**; only the ack/clear/doorbell side effects run (and the ROM zeros make the cbz NULL handler checks safe). This REFUTES the earlier assumption (in several lane reports) that PD4 dispatches through the real vendor handler table: the DUPLEX SUCCESS (calibration answers, ctx+0x190 allocation) therefore happens through a NON-MAILBOX path - consistent with the ETE SR engine delivering our H2D frames into device memory and the firmware's pcie_msg_init polled H2D thread processing them (the mailbox ack is bookkeeping). PD4-NOSENT would crash with an empty table; PD4-ALWAYS0 equals the current behavior. This is a citable correction for the RE report (fw-audit H1/H2 answered; re-report2's 6.6 section needs updating). ALSO: bench2 fixed the phase-matrix gate bug (opensource 5d93b4d): the generated gate helpers grepped a hard-coded repo path instead of the passed pattern (template edit artifact) so EVERY attr/output gate failed; now helpers use $p + whitespace normalization, host evaluator _search_pattern_ws, regression tests (selftest 398/398), C2 dry-run PLAN-OK; replay of the failed fire passes load-budget.
